<!-- BEGIN SOURCE FILE 18: ReadMeHub_Complete_Project_Files/01_Project_Documents/Claude_Project_MD_Files/03_ELEMENTOR_IMPLEMENTATION_RULES.md -->
### Source file 18: `ReadMeHub_Complete_Project_Files/01_Project_Documents/Claude_Project_MD_Files/03_ELEMENTOR_IMPLEMENTATION_RULES.md`

# ELEMENTOR_IMPLEMENTATION_RULES.md

## Goal
Implement Google Stitch screenshots as accurate, editable, responsive sections in the existing ReadMeHub WordPress staging site.

## Priority
1. Native Elementor widgets and containers.
2. Existing reusable templates and global styles.
3. Installed and verified Xpro widgets.
4. WordPress core features and existing ACF fields.
5. Minimal scoped custom CSS.
6. Site-specific custom code only when necessary and authorized.

## Native-first requirements
Use editable containers and appropriate widgets such as headings, text, images, buttons, icons, lists, dividers, accordions, and supported forms. Use loop/archive features only if the installed edition/addons support them.

Do not put a whole page or major section into one HTML widget. Do not use an iframe or screenshot as a substitute for real content. Do not assume premium features are installed. Do not claim edits happened without verified execution.

## Before editing
1. Identify target page and section.
2. Inspect existing Elementor structure and adjacent sections.
3. Determine whether the component is reused elsewhere.
4. Check available widgets, global styles, and templates.
5. Identify data sources and interactions.
6. Prepare a concise plan.
7. Make only authorized changes.

## CSS and code
Keep CSS scoped to the target section/component. Prefer existing tokens. Avoid broad selectors, unnecessary `!important`, fixed heights that clip content, and fragile positioning. Document where CSS is added. Obtain authorization before installing plugins, editing PHP, or making high-impact shared changes.

## Editability
Text, images, links, buttons, and repeated cards should remain editable in Elementor wherever practical. Use reusable templates when supported. Explain any custom feature that reduces editability.

## Responsive QA
Check 1440px, 1280px, 768px, 390px, and 360px where practical. Verify wrapping, grid behavior, navigation, image cropping, button alignment, overflow, and footer behavior. Do not report tests as passed unless performed.

## Required report
Target page/section; widgets and containers; reusable components; ACF/dynamic data; CSS/code/plugin dependencies; responsive checks; verification evidence; known differences; blockers. If direct WordPress access is unavailable, say so and provide a supported alternative.
 
<!-- END SOURCE FILE 18: ReadMeHub_Complete_Project_Files/01_Project_Documents/Claude_Project_MD_Files/03_ELEMENTOR_IMPLEMENTATION_RULES.md -->

---

<!-- BEGIN SOURCE FILE 19: ReadMeHub_Complete_Project_Files/01_Project_Documents/Claude_Project_MD_Files/04_WORDPRESS_ACF_CONTENT_MAP.md -->
### Source file 19: `ReadMeHub_Complete_Project_Files/01_Project_Documents/Claude_Project_MD_Files/04_WORDPRESS_ACF_CONTENT_MAP.md`

# WORDPRESS_ACF_CONTENT_MAP.md

## Purpose
Document how ReadMeHub content is stored in WordPress and rendered through Elementor. Populate this file from actual site inspection; proposed structures are not confirmation of existing functionality.

## Historical baseline
Previously reported: WordPress, Hello Elementor, Elementor, ACF, Rank Math SEO, and Xpro Elementor Addons/Theme Builder. Confirm current versions, editions, and capabilities.

## Inspect existing content
Record actual findings:
- Posts, pages, categories, tags, media usage
- Existing custom post types: display name, slug, archive/public status, supports, rewrite rules, registration source
- Existing taxonomies: name, slug, associated post types, hierarchy, registration source

## ACF field inventory
For every field group, record title/key, location rules, field label/name/type, return format, required/optional status, default value, conditional logic, and current usage.

Do not assume ACF Repeater or Flexible Content is available. Confirm the installed edition and feature support.

## Potential content model — proposal only
Depending on actual architecture, the site may use:
- Standard Posts for articles and guides
- Tools CPT for tool reviews/directories
- Comparisons CPT for comparisons
- Deals/Coupons CPT for offers

Potential fields: tool logo, official URL, short description, pricing/source/date, features, limitations, related tools, comparison relationships/criteria, coupon code, offer destination/terms/expiry, verification status, last verified date.

These are suggestions, not confirmation that fields or CPTs exist.

## Elementor dynamic mapping
For each template, document the WordPress object/query, dynamic title/content/image, taxonomy, ACF fields, related-content query, conditional display rules, missing-field behavior, and Elementor/addon dependencies.

## Change control
Inspect before creating CPTs, taxonomies, or fields. Avoid duplicates. Use native WordPress fields when sufficient. Document significant content-model changes and obtain approval. Hide optional sections when data is missing. Never fabricate ratings, prices, discounts, codes, expiry dates, or verification claims.
 
<!-- END SOURCE FILE 19: ReadMeHub_Complete_Project_Files/01_Project_Documents/Claude_Project_MD_Files/04_WORDPRESS_ACF_CONTENT_MAP.md -->

---

<!-- BEGIN SOURCE FILE 20: ReadMeHub_Complete_Project_Files/01_Project_Documents/Claude_Project_MD_Files/05_IMPLEMENTATION_LOG_AND_QA.md -->
### Source file 20: `ReadMeHub_Complete_Project_Files/01_Project_Documents/Claude_Project_MD_Files/05_IMPLEMENTATION_LOG_AND_QA.md`

# IMPLEMENTATION_LOG_AND_QA.md

## Purpose
Maintain a factual record of work on the ReadMeHub staging website. Update after each task.

## Safety
- Authorized target: staging only.
- Production must remain untouched.
- Record high-impact changes and rollback options.
- Never mark a task complete without actual verification.

## Task log template
### Task [number] — [section]
- Date:
- Target page URL:
- Reference screenshot/file:
- Task type: New section / Replacement / Adjustment / Reusable component
- Existing structure inspected:
- Planned implementation:
- Actual changes:
- Elementor widgets/containers:
- Reusable components:
- ACF fields/dynamic data:
- Custom CSS location:
- Custom code/plugin dependencies:
- Responsive viewports inspected:
- Accessibility checks:
- Visual comparison result:
- Functional verification:
- Evidence:
- Known differences:
- Rollback/recovery notes:
- Status: Planned / In progress / Implemented, not fully verified / Verified / Blocked
- Next action:

## QA checklist
### Visual
- [ ] Layout/alignment resembles the reference.
- [ ] Approved colors and typography are preserved.
- [ ] Spacing and container widths are consistent.
- [ ] Images, icons, buttons, and cards render correctly.
- [ ] Header/footer and neighboring sections remain intact.

### Elementor editability
- [ ] Text is editable.
- [ ] Images are editable.
- [ ] Buttons and links are editable.
- [ ] Repeated content uses reusable structures where practical.
- [ ] No full-page HTML-widget shortcut was introduced.

### Dynamic content
- [ ] Data source confirmed.
- [ ] ACF names and return formats confirmed.
- [ ] Missing optional values handled gracefully.
- [ ] No unsupported facts presented as verified.

### Responsive
- [ ] 1440px desktop checked where practical.
- [ ] 1280px laptop checked where practical.
- [ ] 768px tablet checked where practical.
- [ ] 390px mobile checked where practical.
- [ ] 360px small mobile checked where practical.
- [ ] No obvious overflow or clipped content.

### Safety/regression
- [ ] Staging URL confirmed.
- [ ] Production untouched.
- [ ] No unrelated sections changed.
- [ ] Shared templates/global styles checked for impact.
- [ ] Rollback approach considered for significant edits.

## Reporting standard
Separate verified facts, changes actually performed, tests actually run, inferences, blockers, and recommended next actions. Do not claim success based solely on generated code or a plan.
 
<!-- END SOURCE FILE 20: ReadMeHub_Complete_Project_Files/01_Project_Documents/Claude_Project_MD_Files/05_IMPLEMENTATION_LOG_AND_QA.md -->

---

<!-- BEGIN SOURCE FILE 21: ReadMeHub_Complete_Project_Files/01_Project_Documents/Claude_Project_MD_Files/06_CLAUDE_PROJECT_INSTRUCTIONS.md -->
### Source file 21: `ReadMeHub_Complete_Project_Files/01_Project_Documents/Claude_Project_MD_Files/06_CLAUDE_PROJECT_INSTRUCTIONS.md`

# CLAUDE PROJECT INSTRUCTIONS — READMEHUB ELEMENTOR IMPLEMENTATION AGENT

## Role and mission
Act as a senior WordPress implementation engineer, Elementor native-widget specialist, screenshot-to-website reconstruction expert, ACF specialist, responsive QA engineer, and change-control specialist.

I will provide Google Stitch screenshots one section at a time. Reconstruct each in the existing ReadMeHub WordPress staging site using editable Elementor components and the approved design system. This is not a new branding project.

## Target and safety
Staging: `https://www.readmehub.com/stagging`
Only staging is authorized. Never modify production.

Previously reported: Hello Elementor, Elementor 4.2.4, ACF 6.8.9, Rank Math SEO, Xpro Addons/Theme Builder, Elementor Pro not installed. Verify actual versions and capabilities before relying on them.

Do not delete content, run destructive database operations, replace the theme, install plugins, change DNS/hosting, edit PHP, or modify shared templates/global styles without appropriate authorization. Never expose credentials or secrets.

## Screenshot protocol
For each screenshot:
1. Identify target page and section.
2. Inspect current page and Elementor structure.
3. Inspect nearby sections, global styles, templates, and data sources.
4. Determine layout and hierarchy; label estimates clearly.
5. Choose the least disruptive implementation.
6. Implement only the authorized section.
7. Inspect the actual result.
8. Report changes and differences.

Ask one concise question if the target is genuinely ambiguous.

## Native-first implementation
Priority: native Elementor widgets/containers; existing reusable templates/global styles; installed and verified Xpro widgets; WordPress functionality and existing ACF; minimal scoped CSS; custom code only when necessary and authorized.

Do not put a whole page or major section in one HTML widget. Do not use a screenshot or iframe instead of real content. Do not assume premium features are available.

## Editability and design system
Keep text, images, buttons, links, and repeated cards editable wherever practical. Reuse templates when supported. Preserve the approved palette, typography, spacing, cards, buttons, header, footer, radii, shadows, and responsive behavior. Do not change global styles to solve local issues.

## WordPress and ACF
Inspect the content model before using or creating CPTs, taxonomies, or ACF fields. Confirm field names, types, return formats, and location rules. Do not assume ACF Repeater or Flexible Content is available. Avoid duplicates.

## Responsive and accessibility
Check 1440px, 1280px, 768px, 390px, and 360px where practical. Verify wrapping, grids, navigation, images, buttons, overflow, and footer. Use semantic headings, visible focus, accessible labels, sufficient contrast, meaningful alt text, and clear states. Aim for WCAG 2.2 AA where practical.

## Verification and reporting
After each task, report page/section, inspection, changes, widgets, ACF/dynamic data, CSS/code/plugin dependencies, responsive checks, evidence, differences, and blockers. Distinguish verified, unverified, blocked, and not implemented. Never claim a change occurred without evidence.

If editing access is unavailable, say so and provide a supported alternative. Work on one section at a time. Prioritize fidelity, editability, safety, maintainability, and verifiable results.
 
<!-- END SOURCE FILE 21: ReadMeHub_Complete_Project_Files/01_Project_Documents/Claude_Project_MD_Files/06_CLAUDE_PROJECT_INSTRUCTIONS.md -->

---

<!-- BEGIN SOURCE FILE 22: ReadMeHub_Complete_Project_Files/01_Project_Documents/Claude_Project_MD_Files/07_PER_SECTION_TASK_PROMPT.md -->
### Source file 22: `ReadMeHub_Complete_Project_Files/01_Project_Documents/Claude_Project_MD_Files/07_PER_SECTION_TASK_PROMPT.md`

# PER-SECTION TASK PROMPT — READMEHUB

Implement the attached Google Stitch screenshot in my existing ReadMeHub WordPress staging website.

Target staging URL: `https://www.readmehub.com/stagging`

Follow the permanent Claude Project Instructions and available project context files.

## 1. Identify
Identify the target page and section. Determine whether it is a new section, replacement, adjustment, or reusable component. If ambiguous, inspect the site/context first and ask one concise question only if necessary.

## 2. Inspect
Inspect the current page, Elementor structure, nearby sections, global styles, reusable templates, and relevant ACF data. Confirm which widgets and addon features are actually available.

## 3. Plan
State a concise plan identifying the least disruptive implementation approach. Do not start unrelated work.

## 4. Implement
Match the screenshot's layout, hierarchy, spacing, typography, colors, imagery, borders, shadows, cards, and CTA placement while preserving the approved design system.

Use editable Elementor containers and native widgets first. Reuse global styles and components. Use existing ACF fields when confirmed. Avoid one HTML widget for the entire section.

## 5. Preserve truth
Do not fabricate ratings, prices, discounts, reviews, testimonials, authors, expiry dates, or verification claims. Use actual content or clearly identified placeholders.

## 6. Responsive
Implement suitable tablet/mobile behavior. Check 1440px, 1280px, 768px, 390px, and 360px where practical. Do not claim a viewport was tested unless it was inspected.

## 7. Verify and report
Report:
1. Target page and section
2. Existing structure inspected
3. Widgets and containers used
4. Components reused
5. ACF/dynamic data involved
6. Custom CSS/code/plugin dependencies
7. Responsive checks performed
8. Verification evidence
9. Differences from the screenshot
10. Remaining blockers

If authorized WordPress access and editing tools are available, implement the section rather than merely describing it. If not, state the limitation and provide a supported alternative.

Only modify staging. Do not change production.
 
<!-- END SOURCE FILE 22: ReadMeHub_Complete_Project_Files/01_Project_Documents/Claude_Project_MD_Files/07_PER_SECTION_TASK_PROMPT.md -->

<!-- BEGIN SOURCE FILE 23: ReadMeHub_Complete_Project_Files/01_Project_Documents/Claude_Project_MD_Files/08_READMEHUB_PAGE_TEMPLATE_SCOPE.md -->
### Source file 23: `ReadMeHub_Complete_Project_Files/01_Project_Documents/Claude_Project_MD_Files/08_READMEHUB_PAGE_TEMPLATE_SCOPE.md`

# READMEHUB PAGE AND TEMPLATE SCOPE

## Purpose
Plan the templates extending the approved homepage. This is a scope checklist, not proof that any template already exists.

## Required templates
1. AI Tools / Software Directory
2. Single Tool Review
3. Blog Archive
4. Single Blog Article
5. Category Archive
6. Comparisons Archive
7. Single Comparison
8. Deals and Coupons Archive
9. Single Deal or Coupon
10. Search Results
11. About ReadMeHub
12. Editorial Methodology
13. Affiliate Disclosure
14. Contact and Report an Error
15. Privacy Policy
16. Terms of Service
17. Custom 404

The approved homepage remains unchanged unless explicitly authorized.

## Shared components
Reuse existing components where available: header/navigation, mobile navigation, footer, search, breadcrumbs, page introduction, section headings, category navigation, tool/article/comparison/deal cards, editorial metadata, verification badges, CTA buttons, newsletter panel, pagination, filters, comparison table, form fields, empty states, related content, and affiliate disclosure notice.

Do not duplicate a component with slightly different styling without a functional reason.

## Template notes
- Directory: searchable/filterable tool cards and useful empty states.
- Tool review: overview, features, limitations, pricing source, use cases, alternatives, related content, methodology.
- Blog archive: featured article, categories, article cards, pagination.
- Single article: readable content width, metadata, optional table of contents, sources, related content.
- Category archive: relevant introduction and category-specific content.
- Comparisons archive: comparison cards and filters.
- Single comparison: criteria, readable table, source information, use cases, alternatives, mobile table strategy.
- Deals archive: verification status, terms, expiry, filters, active/expired/unverified states.
- Single deal: terms, code only when real, destination, verification metadata, disclosure.
- Search results: content types, filters, loading and no-results states.
- About: mission and actual supplied information only.
- Methodology: transparent research, verification, update, and correction policies.
- Affiliate disclosure: plain-language commercial relationship explanation.
- Contact/report error: accessible forms and success/error states.
- Privacy/terms: readable legal-document templates; actual policy content needs owner/legal confirmation.
- 404: helpful search, homepage action, relevant category links.

## Trust and data integrity
Never invent ratings, reviews, testimonials, product prices, discounts, coupon codes, expiry dates, author credentials, company history, user counts, or testing claims. Optional data should disappear gracefully. Unverified offers must not look verified; expired offers must not appear active.

## Implementation handoff
For every template, map WordPress object/query, Elementor structure, dynamic title/content/image, taxonomy, ACF fields, related content, conditional visibility, empty state, plugin/edition dependencies, and responsive behavior.
 
<!-- END SOURCE FILE 23: ReadMeHub_Complete_Project_Files/01_Project_Documents/Claude_Project_MD_Files/08_READMEHUB_PAGE_TEMPLATE_SCOPE.md -->

<!-- BEGIN SOURCE FILE 24: ReadMeHub_Complete_Project_Files/01_Project_Documents/ReadMeHub Research and Architecture Foundation.md -->
### Source file 24: `ReadMeHub_Complete_Project_Files/01_Project_Documents/ReadMeHub Research and Architecture Foundation.md`

# ReadMeHub Research and Architecture Foundation

**Prepared by:** Manus AI  
**Date:** 6 September 2026  
**Status:** Foundation phase complete; live WordPress implementation pending environment access and launch-category data.

## 1. Executive summary

ReadMeHub should be built as an **evidence-led decision platform**, not as a generic affiliate blog. Its central job is to help a reader understand what options exist, which option fits a particular situation, what the relevant trade-offs and costs are, and what action is reasonable next. The recommended product model combines editorial publication, research platform, comparison engine, buying guide, and verified-offer directory.

The architecture should be broad enough to support software and physical products, but the first publishing wedge should be narrow. The supplied brief does not identify a launch category, audience, product set, affiliate network, keyword export, or live WordPress installation. Therefore, this document defines a category-neutral system and uses query patterns rather than invented search volumes, rankings, prices, ratings, discounts, or affiliate relationships.

Public competitor research shows that leading platforms connect categories, product pages, reviews, alternatives, comparisons, and decision assistance. G2 presents a workflow built around alternatives and side-by-side comparisons using verified-user review signals.[1] Capterra combines category discovery, product profiles, reviews, ratings, methodology, and commercial disclosures.[2] AlternativeTo demonstrates the value of structured alternative discovery using categories, platform availability, pricing models, user opinions, and related software relationships.[3]

ReadMeHub’s clearest defensible advantage is **transparent decision rationale**: every recommendation should explain fit, anti-fit, evidence, price context, limitations, alternatives, regional conditions, and the date on which volatile facts were verified.

## 2. Scope, assumptions, and evidence boundaries

The requested production stack is WordPress with Hello Elementor, Elementor, ACF, lightweight custom CSS/JavaScript, and only necessary SEO, performance, analytics, newsletter, and affiliate infrastructure. No WordPress installation, staging environment, credentials, backup, keyword export, analytics, or affiliate data was available during this foundation phase. No live-site changes have been made.

| Area | Current state | Foundation decision |
|---|---|---|
| Market | Global ambition; priority markets are the United States, Canada, United Kingdom, and Australia | Design region-aware records without creating duplicate country pages |
| Vertical | Not supplied | Build category-neutral architecture; select one deep launch wedge before publishing at scale |
| Entity mix | Software and products are both mentioned | Support both, but use one dominant entity model for the first launch |
| Search data | No keyword or SERP export supplied | Use intent families and query patterns; do not claim volume or difficulty |
| WordPress | No installation access | Specify implementation; defer audit and configuration |
| Commercial data | No partner or offer data supplied | Do not publish affiliate claims, prices, coupons, or discounts until verified |
| Trust model | Brief requires transparency and no fabrication | Make evidence, methodology, authorship, disclosures, and update dates first-class data |

A **software-first** launch would require plan, seat, integration, usage-limit, security, trial, and billing fields. A **physical-product-first** launch would require variant, specification, availability, shipping, warranty, return, and retailer-offer fields. Mixing both deeply in the first wedge would increase operational complexity and weaken editorial coherence.

## 3. Strategic positioning and differentiation

### Positioning statement

> ReadMeHub helps people make better product and software decisions by combining independent editorial analysis, structured comparisons, practical use-case guidance, transparent pricing context, and verified offers.

### Decision questions the site must answer

| User question | ReadMeHub response | Primary page families |
|---|---|---|
| What kind of solution is this? | Category definition, use cases, and selection criteria | Categories, guides, use cases |
| Which options exist? | Structured products, brands, and category discovery | Categories, products, brands |
| Which option fits my situation? | Explicit fit, anti-fit, and decision criteria | Reviews, comparisons, use cases |
| What will it cost? | Plan or product pricing, total-cost notes, currency and regional context | Pricing, reviews, offers |
| What should I do next? | Clear, disclosed next action plus alternatives | Reviews, comparisons, deals, coupons |

### Competitor-derived opportunity map

| Observed reference model | Public strength | ReadMeHub response |
|---|---|---|
| G2 | Connected product, alternative, category, review, and comparison workflow; comparison pages describe 2–4 products and verified-user review signals.[1] | Add editorial decision rules, anti-fit guidance, evidence records, use-case segmentation, pricing interpretation, and a plain-language verdict |
| Capterra | Category discovery, profiles, ratings, reviews, research methodology, review verification, and visible sponsored/referral disclosures.[2] | Place commercial context and recommendation logic close to the recommendation, not only in a footer; separate user feedback from editorial judgment |
| AlternativeTo | Large alternatives graph with categories, platforms, pricing models, open-source labels, and user-opinion signals.[3] | Add stronger reasons for switching, migration risks, total-cost context, audience fit, and editorial verification |

These are observations of public page structures, not claims about traffic, rankings, conversion rates, or business performance.

## 4. Search-intent architecture

ReadMeHub should map one canonical page to each underlying decision. Minor wording variations should consolidate into one useful page instead of producing doorway pages or near-identical country pages.

| Intent family | Example query pattern | Funnel stage | Canonical page | Supporting links | Commercial route |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Discovery | best [category]; [category] tools | Consideration | Category hub or buyer guide | Use cases, reviews, comparisons, pricing | Contextual provider/product links |
| Product evaluation | [product] review | Decision | Product review | Pricing, alternatives, methodology | Disclosed outbound action |
| Comparison | [product A] vs [product B] | Decision | Comparison | Both reviews, category, pricing | Choice-specific links |
| Alternatives | [product] alternatives | Decision | Alternatives page | Product review, migration guide, category | Substitute options |
| Pricing | [product] pricing; [category] pricing comparison | Decision | Pricing page or module | Review, plans, total-cost guide | Provider/product link |
| Use case | best [category] for [audience/use case] | Consideration | Use-case guide | Category, reviews, comparisons | Segmented recommendations |
| Deal | [product] deal; [brand] offer | Transaction | Deal page | Product, pricing, terms | Verified offer link |
| Coupon | [brand] coupon; [product] promo code | Transaction | Coupon page | Offer terms, product review | Verified coupon link |
| Education | how to choose [category]; what is [category] | Awareness | Guide | Glossary, category, comparisons | Soft conversion path |
| Entity | [brand] products; [brand] review | Discovery/decision | Brand hub | Products, reviews, category | Product or provider links |

Each brief should record primary intent, secondary intent, audience, country/region, funnel stage, commercial value, SERP pattern, recommended content type, canonical target, parent topic, supporting topics, internal links, affiliate opportunity, and offer opportunity.

## 5. GEO and answer-engine readiness

GEO should be integrated into ordinary editorial quality rather than treated as a separate collection of low-value pages. Important pages should make the answer easy to extract while preserving deeper analysis.

Every major decision page should begin with a concise summary that states the verdict, the best-fit audience, the principal limitation, the current pricing context, and the date of verification. It should then provide a comparison table, pros and cons, who should use the option, who should avoid it, alternatives, methodology, sources, and update information.

| GEO requirement | Implementation rule |
|---|---|
| Direct answer | Answer the page’s primary question in the opening section |
| Entity clarity | Use consistent names for product, brand, category, plan, region, and use case |
| Evidence | Attach claims to source URLs, source type, checked date, region, and reviewer |
| Extractability | Use descriptive headings, short paragraphs, tables, lists, and explicit definitions |
| Original value | Add decision frameworks, trade-offs, anti-fit guidance, synthesis, and interpretation |
| Regional relevance | Add currency, availability, taxes, compliance, shipping, and plan differences only when materially different |
| Freshness | Display a meaningful reviewed date tied to claim revalidation |
| Trust | Identify author, reviewer, methodology, corrections route, and commercial relationship |

The site should not generate large numbers of pages that merely change a country name or product name. Programmatic publication should be blocked when a page lacks verified data, a clear intent, a valid comparison set, or unique editorial guidance.

## 6. Information architecture and URL model

The primary navigation should be task-led: **Reviews, Compare, Alternatives, Pricing, Deals, and Guides**. Category and product browsing should be available through hubs and contextual navigation, but users should not be required to understand the taxonomy before beginning a decision.

| Section | Suggested URL | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| Reviews | `/reviews/` | Editorial product and software assessments |
| Comparisons | `/comparisons/` | Direct comparison of two to four options |
| Alternatives | `/alternatives/` | Replacement and switching decisions |
| Guides | `/guides/` | Selection, education, setup, and buying guidance |
| Deals | `/deals/` | Verified limited-time or evergreen offers |
| Coupons | `/coupons/` | Verified codes and coupon terms |
| Products | `/products/` | Stable product/entity records |
| Brands | `/brands/` | Vendor and brand hubs |
| Use cases | `/use-cases/` | Audience, role, workflow, or problem-led recommendations |
| Categories | `/categories/` | Topic and solution hubs |
| Pricing | `/pricing/` | Pricing references and total-cost analysis |
| Authors | `/authors/` | Editorial identity and expertise |
| Methodology | `/how-we-evaluate/` | Scoring, evidence, testing, and update method |
| Editorial policy | `/editorial-policy/` | Standards, corrections, and independence |
| Affiliate disclosure | `/affiliate-disclosure/` | Commercial relationships and compensation explanation |
| Contact | `/contact/` | Corrections, partnerships, and reader contact |

Canonical entity pages should remain stable while editorial articles and offers change. Archives should not be indexable until they contain unique, useful content. Expired offers should be archived or clearly marked according to the final search policy.

## 7. Taxonomy and entity graph

The site should use WordPress native post types and taxonomies for indexable editorial content, while ACF supplies structured fields and relationships. The article body should remain ordinary WordPress content rather than being stored entirely in custom fields.

### Recommended content types

| Content type | Role |
|---|---|
| Review | Editorial assessment of a product or software entity |
| Comparison | Structured comparison of two to four products |
| Alternative Set | Replacement options anchored to a product or category |
| Guide | Buyer, educational, setup, or decision content |
| Deal | Verified offer with terms and validity state |
| Coupon | Verified code or promotion with terms and expiration state |
| Pricing Reference | Pricing and total-cost interpretation |
| News/Update | Freshness-sensitive changes only when editorially justified |

### Core taxonomies

Category, use case, audience/role, brand, region, product type, buying criterion, pricing model, platform, and topic cluster. Products and plans should be relational records rather than uncontrolled tag values.

### Relationship model

A category contains products, brands, use cases, guides, reviews, comparisons, alternatives, pricing references, deals, and coupons. A product belongs to one or more categories and a brand, supports plans or variants, and can appear in reviews, comparisons, alternatives, offers, and use cases. A comparison connects two to four products and records criteria and decision logic. An evidence record supports one or more claims. An offer applies to a product or brand and is constrained by region, currency, dates, terms, and source.

## 8. ACF data architecture

ACF should model structured decision data, not replace WordPress editorial writing. Every volatile or commercial claim should be traceable to an evidence record.

| Field group | Required fields | Important relationships |
|---|---|---|
| Product | Name, slug, short description, product type, brand, categories, official URL, logo/image, availability, platforms, pricing model, editorial status | Brand, categories, use cases, plans, reviews, offers, evidence |
| Brand | Name, description, official URL, headquarters/region if verified, support/contact URLs, logo, disclosure notes | Products, categories, evidence, offers |
| Review | Verdict, fit summary, anti-fit summary, editorial score only if rubric exists, pros, cons, criteria, author, reviewer, reviewed date, update date, methodology, sources, disclosure | Product, category, use cases, comparisons, alternatives, evidence |
| Comparison | Product A/B/C/D, primary question, criteria, criterion weights if used, verdict, best for each audience, winner by criterion, limitations, date checked, sources, disclosure | Products, category, use cases, pricing, evidence |
| Alternative Set | Anchor product/category, switching reason, recommended alternatives, migration notes, trade-offs, best-for labels, data date, sources | Product, category, alternatives, comparisons, reviews |
| Guide | Intent, audience, decision stage, summary answer, criteria, steps, FAQ, author, reviewer, update date, sources | Category, use cases, products, reviews |
| Offer | Product/brand, offer type, code if verified, landing URL, region, currency, terms, start date, expiry date, checked date, status, source, partner ID if applicable | Product, brand, region, evidence |
| Pricing Plan/Variant | Name, billing cadence, price, currency, included limits, seats/quantity, trial, renewal notes, region, checked date, source | Product, evidence, offers |
| Evidence | Claim supported, source URL, source type, source title, checked date, region, currency, confidence, reviewer, excerpt/notes, expiration/revalidation date | Claims, products, offers, pages |
| Author/Reviewer | Name, role, biography, expertise, profile image, credentials/experience, social or professional links, editorial role | Reviews, guides, comparisons |
| Category | Definition, audience, selection criteria, featured products, use cases, related guides, comparisons, alternatives, pricing notes, region notes | All content types |

### Evidence status values

Use controlled values such as **Verified, Needs recheck, Expired, Disputed, Unavailable, and Editorial judgment**. A page should not display “verified” unless the corresponding source and checked date exist.

### Editorial score rule

Do not create a proprietary score until ReadMeHub has a published rubric, criterion definitions, weighting method, reviewer responsibility, and evidence basis. User ratings, editorial scores, and market signals must remain separate and clearly labeled.

## 9. Elementor design and template system

Hello Elementor should remain the active theme. Elementor should provide visual construction, reusable sections, global styles, and templates. ACF should supply dynamic structured data. Custom CSS/JavaScript should be limited to interactions and layout needs that Elementor cannot handle cleanly.

| Template | Required modules |
|---|---|
| Global header | Logo, task-led navigation, search, disclosure/accessibility links |
| Global footer | Primary navigation, trust pages, newsletter, contact, privacy, terms |
| Category archive | Definition, decision criteria, featured options, use cases, guides, comparisons, pricing, latest updates |
| Product single | Verdict, fit/anti-fit, key facts, pricing, evidence, pros/cons, alternatives, reviews, offers, methodology |
| Review single | Verdict card, score/rubric if valid, criteria, evidence, pros/cons, audience fit, pricing, alternatives, disclosure |
| Comparison single | At-a-glance table, criteria, verdict, best-for cards, trade-offs, pricing, evidence, related reviews |
| Alternatives single | Anchor context, reasons to switch, alternative cards, migration risks, comparison links |
| Guide single | Direct answer, contents, criteria, analysis, product paths, FAQs, sources, author/reviewer |
| Deal/coupon single | Offer status, terms, region, validity, source, redemption path, related review, disclosure |
| Trust module | Author, reviewer, methodology, last verified date, corrections/contact link |
| Newsletter module | Value proposition, consent language, privacy link, low-friction form |

The visual system should use restrained editorial typography, strong hierarchy, generous whitespace, accessible contrast, compact comparison tables, consistent source/date treatments, and mobile-first cards. Affiliate actions should be visually clear but should not overpower the editorial verdict.

## 10. Internal linking architecture

Internal linking should follow the entity graph and the reader’s decision sequence rather than exhaustive sitewide blocks.

| Source page | Link destinations |
|---|---|
| Category hub | Reviews, comparisons, use cases, pricing, guides, alternatives |
| Review | Product, brand, category, pricing, comparisons, alternatives, offers, methodology |
| Comparison | Each product review, category, use cases, pricing, alternatives |
| Alternatives | Anchor product, alternative reviews, comparisons, migration guide, category |
| Guide | Category, criteria, use cases, reviews, comparisons, methodology |
| Offer | Product, review, pricing, terms, disclosure, related alternatives |
| Brand | Products, category hubs, reviews, offers, evidence-aware updates |

Use descriptive anchor text, one canonical page per intent, breadcrumb navigation, related-content modules with a clear reason for inclusion, and contextual links inside the body. Avoid orphaned pages, generic “click here” anchors, and large repeated link inventories.

## 11. SEO, schema, and technical foundation

The implementation should include clean permalinks, one canonical URL per intent, XML sitemaps for indexable content, controlled robots directives, Open Graph metadata, descriptive titles and descriptions, image dimensions and alt text, responsive layouts, and a measured performance budget. The final schema implementation must be validated against current search guidance before production.

| Page/entity | Schema direction |
|---|---|
| Publisher | Organization and Person where identity is available |
| Editorial article | Article or BlogPosting when appropriate |
| Review | Review only when a genuine review basis, item, author, and visible review content exist |
| Product/offer | Product and Offer only when visible facts, price, currency, availability, and source are current |
| Navigation | BreadcrumbList |
| FAQ | FAQPage only for visible, genuine FAQs and where current eligibility is satisfied |
| Comparison | Article plus visible comparison content; do not invent unsupported aggregate review schema |

Schema must never contain data that is absent from visible page content. Ratings, prices, availability, discounts, and review values must be synchronized with their evidence records.

## 12. Affiliate, deals, and newsletter UX

Commercial relationships should be disclosed in plain language near the relevant recommendation and linked to a complete affiliate-disclosure page. The public Capterra model demonstrates why methodology, review verification, sponsored-profile context, and referral-fee disclosure should be treated as user-facing trust features.[2]

Deals and coupons require an operational owner, source URL, region, currency, terms, start/check/expiry state, and revalidation cadence. If an offer cannot be verified, it should not be presented as active. A coupon page without a current valid offer should be clearly marked, archived, or excluded from indexing under the final SEO policy.

The newsletter should promise a specific value, request only necessary consent, link to privacy terms, and avoid using urgency or manipulative copy. A suitable initial promise is a periodic digest of researched comparisons, price changes, and verified decision guides—not a generic promotional mailing list.

## 13. Launch sequence

The first launch should be deep rather than broad. Choose the initial vertical using evidence availability, commercial coverage, differentiation potential, audience clarity, and operational maintainability rather than apparent search demand alone.

| Sequence | Deliverable | Publication gate |
|---:|---|---|
| 1 | Methodology, editorial policy, affiliate disclosure, About, contact/corrections | Trust and governance pages are complete |
| 2 | One category hub and one buyer guide | Clear category definition, criteria, and supporting entities |
| 3 | Three to five verified product or software reviews | Evidence records, authorship, fit/anti-fit, pricing context |
| 4 | Three to five comparisons | At least two verified options and explicit criteria |
| 5 | One alternatives page for a major incumbent or category anchor | Switching rationale and trade-offs are documented |
| 6 | Two to four use-case pages | Each serves a distinct audience/problem and links to products |
| 7 | Pricing or total-cost reference | Current source, currency, region, and checked date |
| 8 | Deals and coupons, only if operationally maintainable | Offer terms, validity, disclosure, and revalidation owner |

## 14. Quality-assurance gates

A page should not be indexed or monetized merely because its template renders. The following gates should be automated where possible and manually reviewed where judgment is required.

| QA area | Pass condition |
|---|---|
| Editorial integrity | No fabricated reviews, testing, prices, ratings, statistics, discounts, coupons, or relationships |
| Search intent | One clear primary intent and canonical URL |
| Evidence | Commercial and volatile claims map to dated sources |
| Comparison | At least two verified products and understandable criteria |
| Review | Published rubric and evidence basis exist before a score appears |
| Offers | Region, terms, source, status, and revalidation date are present |
| GEO/AEO | Direct answer, entities, source context, and deeper analysis are all present |
| Accessibility | Keyboard navigation, semantic headings, labels, focus states, contrast, alt text, and responsive behavior pass review |
| Performance | Images optimized, scripts limited, layout stable, and mobile experience tested |
| Schema | Markup reflects visible, accurate content and validates without material errors |
| Affiliate UX | CTA does not obscure verdict, alternatives, limitations, or disclosure |
| Programmatic content | Unique guidance exists beyond substituting a product, country, or keyword |

## 15. Unresolved inputs required for the next phase

The foundation is ready for specialization, but the following inputs are required before category-specific research or implementation:

1. The initial launch vertical: software, consumer technology, home products, finance tools, hosting, or another defined category.
2. The initial audience: consumer, small business, enterprise, creator, developer, professional role, or another segment.
3. The first market and currency priority.
4. The intended affiliate networks, direct partnerships, or non-affiliate monetization model.
5. Any existing keyword exports, analytics, content, product lists, or competitor list.
6. WordPress staging/admin access, backup, or export for the installation audit.
7. The intended editorial team, reviewer credentials, testing capability, and update ownership.

## 16. Foundation conclusion

ReadMeHub should be implemented as a maintained **evidence graph wrapped in an editorial decision experience**. The broad architecture is now defined, but the project should not move directly into mass content production. The correct next step is to choose a narrow launch wedge, obtain the relevant product and affiliate data, validate country-specific search demand, and then implement the WordPress/Hello Elementor/Elementor/ACF system on staging.

This document does not claim that WordPress, Hello Elementor, Elementor, ACF, SEO infrastructure, affiliate systems, or QA have been installed or configured. Those remain implementation tasks pending access to the actual environment.

## References

[1]: https://www.g2.com/compare "G2 Compare: product alternatives and side-by-side comparisons"

[2]: https://www.capterra.com/ "Capterra: software discovery, reviews, methodology, and commercial disclosures"

[3]: https://alternativeto.net/ "AlternativeTo: software alternatives and structured discovery"

[4]: /home/ubuntu/skills/content-strategy/SKILL.md "Content strategy guidance consulted for intent and content planning"

[5]: /home/ubuntu/skills/seo-aeo-best-practices/SKILL.md "SEO and AEO guidance consulted for metadata, structured content, and answer readiness"

**Source note:** G2, Capterra, and AlternativeTo observations are based on publicly accessible pages reviewed on 6 September 2026. Counts, ratings, labels, prices, offers, and other volatile page values should be revalidated before publication.

**Author:** Manus AI

**Document status:** Research and architecture foundation only; live-site audit and implementation pending.
- 

## Appendix A — Category-neutral content brief fields

Every future brief should record the target query family, primary question, audience, region, funnel stage, canonical page, supporting pages, entities, criteria, claims, evidence URLs, data date, author, reviewer, disclosure requirement, internal links, CTA context, and update owner.

## Appendix B — Recommended initial dashboard metrics

Once analytics and search data are available, measure indexed pages by intent, organic impressions and clicks by page family, assisted conversions by content type, outbound click-through rate by disclosed CTA, offer verification age, correction rate, content freshness, and pages failing evidence or schema QA. Do not optimize for affiliate clicks alone; pair commercial metrics with engagement, return visits, reader corrections, and decision-content usefulness.

## Appendix C — Implementation boundary

The supplied target technology remains WordPress, Hello Elementor, Elementor, ACF, and lightweight custom CSS/JavaScript. The next implementation phase should begin with a staging audit of WordPress version, active theme and plugins, Elementor and ACF versions, pages, posts, media, menus, templates, SEO, permalinks, robots, sitemap, schema, performance, caching, analytics, newsletter, and affiliate infrastructure. No assets should be deleted or deactivated without a documented safety review.

> ReadMeHub should not optimize merely to rank or to generate affiliate clicks. It should optimize to become useful enough to deserve ranking and trustworthy enough to support informed decisions.

## References

[1]: https://www.g2.com/compare "G2 Compare"
[2]: https://www.capterra.com/ "Capterra"
[3]: https://alternativeto.net/ "AlternativeTo"
[4]: /home/ubuntu/skills/content-strategy/SKILL.md "Content Strategy guidance"
[5]: /home/ubuntu/skills/seo-aeo-best-practices/SKILL.md "SEO and AEO guidance"

---

**End of foundation document.**

## Appendix D — Source-backed competitor notes

G2 publicly describes a workflow in which a visitor selects a current product, receives alternatives, and compares top choices side by side. It also presents category navigation, trending products, alternatives, and review-backed product comparisons.[1]

Capterra publicly presents category discovery, product profiles, user reviews, ratings, methodology, review verification, sponsored-profile context, and a referral-fee disclosure. These visible trust mechanisms support the recommendation that ReadMeHub expose commercial context and methodology near decision content.[2]

AlternativeTo publicly organizes software alternatives through categories, platforms, pricing models, open-source status, user opinions, and related applications. This supports the recommendation that ReadMeHub treat alternatives as a structured entity relationship rather than a paragraph appended to a review.[3]

These observations are limited to public page structures. They do not establish competitor traffic, rankings, conversion performance, or business results.

## Appendix E — Research limitations and next validation pass

The current foundation did not include a reliable keyword database, country-specific SERP export, backlink dataset, analytics, affiliate agreement, product catalog, or live WordPress audit. The next research pass should therefore validate the chosen launch vertical against actual keyword and SERP data, review at least the leading category-specific publishers, and replace query patterns with sourced keyword records. All implementation decisions should then be tested on staging before production deployment.

## References

[1]: https://www.g2.com/compare "G2 Compare"
[2]: https://www.capterra.com/ "Capterra"
[3]: https://alternativeto.net/ "AlternativeTo"

**Final status:** Foundation delivered. No production implementation has been performed.

<!-- BEGIN SOURCE FILE 25: ReadMeHub_Complete_Project_Files/01_Project_Documents/ReadMeHub — Elementor Free-First Website Specification.md -->
### Source file 25: `ReadMeHub_Complete_Project_Files/01_Project_Documents/ReadMeHub — Elementor Free-First Website Specification.md`

# ReadMeHub — Elementor Free-First Website Specification

**Document type:** Proposed implementation specification and copy-ready build brief; not website implementation, import data, or proof of runtime behavior.  
**Version:** Draft 1.0  
**Project:** ReadMeHub  
**Status:** Target runtime not inspected; launch niche intentionally remains a placeholder.  
**Source of truth:** User-supplied master brief, plus this specification’s explicitly labeled proposals.  
**Owner:** ReadMeHub project owner; implementation and verification owners to be assigned.

> **Important:** No site was built, imported, rendered, or tested for this document. Elementor, WordPress, Hello Elementor, ACF, Xpro, breakpoints, editor behavior, accessibility, and performance remain unverified in the target installation. This file is a handoff specification only.

## 1. Interpretation and intent

Create a maintainable, globally oriented, English-language decision-support publication using WordPress, Hello Elementor, Elementor Free, ACF Free where justified, and only verified free dependencies. The experience should help a focused launch audience understand a problem, explore evidence, compare relevant options, and make a decision. It is editorial-first and may include affiliate links, but commercial actions follow useful information and are clearly disclosed.

**Fidelity intent:** system-first. The supplied brief describes structural principles said to be informed by Jentic research; it explicitly forbids copying Jentic’s visual identity. That research is reported by the brief and was not independently re-fetched for this handoff. The brief’s launch audience/problem is not selected, so copy and examples must remain clearly marked placeholders until the owner supplies real content.

**Primary journey:** Understand → choose a problem → explore evidence → compare options → make a decision → take action.

**Non-goals:** a generic latest-posts homepage, a coupon directory, a 13-category launch portal, a ClickUp landing page, an Elementor Pro build, an import-ready kit, or an unsupported claim of testing, verification, accessibility compliance, production readiness, audience validation, or business performance.

## 2. Evidence, assumptions, blockers, and environment

### 2.1 Evidence labels

- **CONFIRMED** — explicitly supplied in the user’s brief, or directly verified in the target runtime. In this document, “confirmed” normally means supplied by the brief, not independently runtime-verified.
- **LIKELY** — strong inference, pending verification.
- **ASSUMED** — chosen temporarily to close a low-risk gap.
- **UNVERIFIED** — requires inspection or testing.
- **VERSION-DEPENDENT** — depends on installed WordPress, Elementor, theme, license, or add-on version.
- **PROPOSED** — a recommended ReadMeHub design or implementation decision, not a sourced fact.

### 2.2 Target environment register

| Item | Current status | Required verification / owner |
|---|---|---|
| WordPress version | UNVERIFIED | Inspect target admin/system information; implementer |
| Theme | Hello Elementor — CONFIRMED by brief; installed state UNVERIFIED | Confirm active theme and version; implementer |
| Elementor Free/version | Free-only requirement CONFIRMED; installed version UNVERIFIED | Inspect plugin/version; implementer |
| Elementor Pro | Not to be assumed; out of scope | Confirm absent/not used; implementer |
| Elementor dialect | UNVERIFIED (V3 Containers, V4/Atomic, hybrid, legacy) | Inspect editor and available elements before mapping; implementer |
| ACF Free/version | Availability stated in brief; installed/version UNVERIFIED | Confirm field types and license/version; implementer |
| Xpro Addons Free | Availability/version UNVERIFIED | Do not depend on it until installed widgets, license, accessibility, and output are checked |
| Xpro Theme Builder Free | Availability/version UNVERIFIED | Use only if verified in target; otherwise choose a documented Hello-compatible header/footer path |
| Existing site/Kit/globals/templates | Greenfield stated in brief; target state UNVERIFIED | Confirm site is actually new before creating Kit; implementer |
| Site Settings breakpoints | UNVERIFIED | Inspect Elementor → Site Settings → Breakpoints; record exact values |
| Newsletter provider/form | UNVERIFIED | Owner selects a free-tier provider and confirms consent/privacy requirements |
| Launch audience/problem | Intentionally not selected | Owner must choose one audience + problem + use case before publication |
| Palette, font, contrast results | Deferred; no final values | Design-system decision gate, license/performance check, contrast measurement |

### 2.3 Material assumptions and blockers

1. **PROPOSED:** Treat Elementor Free Containers as the starting composition model for a new build. Verify the target dialect and controls first; do not emit widget IDs, control IDs, serialized JSON, dynamic-tag syntax, or import schemas.
2. **PROPOSED:** Keep primary article and editorial content in native WordPress content so it remains crawlable and server-rendered. Use ACF only for stable, structured, reusable values with a genuine editor/query use case.
3. **UNVERIFIED:** Elementor Free’s exact menus, search, post display/query capabilities, Theme Builder alternatives, and any Xpro Free functionality vary by version and installation. Verify every one before implementation.
4. **PROPOSED:** Do not show future categories in public navigation until useful content exists. No empty hubs, placeholder archive grids, or invented inventory.
5. **BLOCKER before launch:** choose the launch cluster, provide real author identities and evidence records, select the newsletter service, finalize jurisdiction-specific legal copy, and settle palette/font after contrast and licensing checks.

## 3. Final information architecture and page relationships

### Launch navigation

**Explore · Guides · Comparisons · Research · About · Search · Newsletter**. No mega-menu. Navigation may omit a label if the corresponding destination is not operational at launch; do not link to empty sections.

### Page relationship map

```text
Home (decision gateway)
├── One Audience / Problem Hub [placeholder until launch niche selected]
│   ├── Use-Case Hub(s) [only where real inventory warrants]
│   ├── Guides / Workflows
│   ├── Comparisons
│   ├── Product / Software entity pages
│   └── Research / decision tools
├── Newsletter
└── Trust / editorial pages
    ├── About
    ├── Methodology
    ├── Editorial Standards
    ├── Affiliate Disclosure
    ├── Privacy
    ├── Terms
    ├── Contact
    └── Corrections / Report an Error
```

**Future-only taxonomy:** Software, Technology, AI, Digital Tools, Business Services, Education/Career, Consumer Technology, and other validated categories. Do not expose these as equal launch hubs until published content exists.

**URL intent rules (PROPOSED):** one canonical URL per primary intent; candidate patterns `/use-case/[audience]/`, `/guides/[task]/`, `/compare/[a]-vs-[b]/`, `/software/[product]/`, `/research/[topic]/`. Finalize after the launch niche and content inventory are set. Avoid duplicate-intent URLs, thin synonym pages, doorway pages, and mass-generated variants.

## 4. Homepage section map — 12 sections

This is a proposed order from the supplied brief. Every module follows the shared rhythm **label → heading → sentence → action** where appropriate. “Real” evidence/content is required before a section is populated; use a designed empty/omitted state rather than fabricated proof or placeholder claims.

| # / section | Purpose and content anatomy | Proposed Elementor composition / responsive behavior | State, editability, and verification |
|---|---|---|---|
| 01 Header | Wordmark, Explore/Guides/Comparisons/Research/About, search, labeled theme control, newsletter or explore action. Mobile: wordmark, search, theme control, menu trigger. | Header template only if a verified free route is available; otherwise a reusable Elementor section/template or theme-compatible implementation. Native containers + verified navigation/search controls; mobile menu behavior must be tested. | Editable nav items and logo. Keyboard order, open/close state, focus, long labels, sticky behavior, and exact template availability need runtime verification. Avoid mega-menu. |
| 02 Hero | Eyebrow, H1 expressing the decision value, supporting sentence, primary and secondary links, nested conceptual route visual: Decision → Compare → Verify → Choose. | Native container(s), Heading, Text Editor, Buttons, and separate editable visual blocks/icons (not a screenshot and not one HTML widget). Two-column on wide screens; copy first, visual second on narrow screens. | Hero text and links editable. Visual is explanatory, not evidence of product tests. One page-level H1. Test no crowding or H1 displacement at intermediate widths. |
| 03 Authority / evidence strip | Only actual editorial attributes: named author/editorial standards/methodology/source practice when genuinely available. No fabricated social proof, counts, testimonials, or trust logos. | Simple row/list of text/icon items, native widgets. Stack/wrap rather than horizontally overflow. | Each claim needs a source/owner; omit unavailable items. Informative icons need text equivalents; decorative icons hidden from assistive tech. |
| 04 “What are you trying to decide?” | 3–4 intent cards routing into real problem/use-case paths; replaces latest-post feed. | Container with card compositions; grid only if supported. 3/2/1 columns are conceptual until breakpoints verified. | Cards contain label, question/need, short guidance, link. Empty inventory means omit or use a transparent “launch focus” message, not dummy cards. |
| 05 Featured workflows | One lead workflow plus 2–4 supporting workflows, with task, outcome, context, evidence state, and link. | Featured split block + repeated card anatomy composed with native widgets. On mobile, lead text before visual; cards stack. | Evidence label must reflect a real record; author/date fields are content-owned. Missing images use a neutral designed fallback, never implied testing imagery. |
| 06 Compare by need | Decision routes organized by job, constraint, workflow, or audience. | Reusable intent-card component; native containers/headings/text/buttons. Responsive stack; do not introduce filters without enough real inventory. | Editable route names and explanatory copy; if no comparison exists, do not link to an empty archive. |
| 07 Featured comparison | A vs B summary with best-for, evidence level, and trade-off for each option. | Two comparison cards or paired native containers, not a giant desktop table. Stack in logical order on mobile. | No score/rating unless methodology and evidence exist. Affiliate CTA, if present, only after useful decision content and with visible disclosure. |
| 08 Original research / data | Real research item, measured question, method cue, dataset/sample only when records exist. | Featured research card + plain-language method indicator, native text/image/icon components. | If there is no original work, omit this section rather than fabricate statistics. Source/method/data ownership recorded. |
| 09 Recently verified / updated | Useful maintained items with “Last verified” or “Verification due” state. | Repeated update cards/list; dates use one fixed site-wide format. | Only show verified dates supported by records. Missing date renders “Not yet verified” only if that wording is editorially approved; otherwise omit the status. Stale records must not look current. |
| 10 Newsletter | Benefit-led invite, email field, clear privacy microcopy; no cadence promise or subscriber count. | Native section and text; form supplied by selected free form/newsletter integration (Elementor Free has no assumed native Form widget). Mobile full-width field and CTA. | Required/consent rules, validation, success/error states, privacy link, data handling, unsubscribe, and provider need verification. Avoid requiring a schedule commitment. |
| 11 Methodology / how we evaluate | Six-step tactile process, evidence ladder, clear link to full methodology. | Six short numbered items in native components; reduce columns and preserve sequence on mobile. | Process wording must match actual editorial practice. No “tested” or “independent” statement absent records. |
| 12 Footer | Primary navigation, trust/legal links, contact/corrections, modest secondary newsletter entry. | Reusable footer pattern; columns become a single ordered stack on mobile. | No oversized link farm. Links must work; legal and corrections routes must exist before publishing relevant claims. |

## 5. Page-template map — 18 families

All are **PROPOSED template definitions**, not proof that Elementor Free can provide global display conditions or query loops. Build only templates needed for the launch inventory. Do not create optional Deals/Offers, Partner, Media Kit, or Submit Product templates until they have an operational purpose.

| # | Family | Core sections / information | Key dynamic or editorial state |
|---|---|---|---|
| 01 | Header | Wordmark, launch navigation, search, labeled theme toggle, one useful CTA; mobile menu | Menu closed/open, focus, current page, search empty/results/error; header mechanism/version needs verification |
| 02 | Footer | Explore, editorial/trust, legal/contact, small newsletter entry | Missing/unpublished links must not render; newsletter integration status |
| 03 | Homepage | The 12-section sequence in §4 | Empty sections omitted; evidence and verification status only from records |
| 04 | Audience / Problem Hub | Breadcrumb, eyebrow/H1/intro, 3–6 decision routes, featured guide, comparison, research/tool, maintained resources, newsletter | Inventory-driven, no fake filters or empty category grid; methodology link |
| 05 | Use-Case Hub | Breadcrumb, use-case promise, routes, featured workflow, comparison preview, related research, maintained resources | One focused intent; filters only when inventory justifies and empty/no-results states exist |
| 06 | Workflow / How-to Article | Breadcrumb, H1/subtitle, author/update/evidence, quick outcome, context, steps, screenshots, failure cases, alternatives, related decision/comparison, sources, newsletter, correction route | Evidence before commercial CTA; screenshots have actual context/capture date where relevant; author/source/date ownership |
| 07 | Comparison | Breadcrumb, A-vs-B H1, decision summary, best-for verdicts, common criteria, detailed comparison, evidence, choose/avoid guidance, alternatives, methodology, last verified, disclosure, final CTA | No giant table requirement; mobile comparison cards; unsupported values get explicit unavailable states |
| 08 | Product / Entity | Name/category/positioning, verification status, at-a-glance facts, best-for/not-ideal, features, sourced pricing, limitations, evidence if present, workflow experience, alternatives, pros/cons, sources, methodology, disclosure, related guides | One canonical entity page per intent. Pricing and “last verified” always source/date-backed. No hands-on claims without records. |
| 09 | Research / Tool | Research label, H1, measured question, why it matters, method, dataset/sample, result, visualization/tool if justified, interpretation, limitations, changelog, sources, related comparisons, newsletter | Build interactive tool only after repeated user need exists. Missing method/sample is not filled with invented data. |
| 10 | Newsletter | Audience, benefits, what is received, no fixed schedule yet, collected data, unsubscribe, privacy, sample issue only if real, FAQ, form | Provider, consent, validation, success, error, and unsubscribe tested before launch |
| 11 | About | Editorial purpose, focus, audience, research approach, update/correction practice, disclosure philosophy, real authors | No invented founder history or credentials |
| 12 | Methodology | Evidence ladder from commodity facts through original/proprietary evidence; process, sourcing, limitations, updates | Levels describe policy; item-level labels require evidence records |
| 13 | Editorial Standards | Evaluation criteria, correction policy, source standards, commercial separation | Policies must be followed operationally; avoid claims broader than practice |
| 14 | Contact | Clear contact purpose and working route/form | If a form is used, selected free provider, spam handling, labels and errors verified |
| 15 | Privacy | Jurisdiction-appropriate data practices, cookies/analytics, newsletter, retention, contact | Legal review/owner required; no invented policy or compliance claim |
| 16 | Terms | Applicable terms for the actual service/site | Jurisdiction/legal review required; not generated as asserted legal advice |
| 17 | Affiliate Disclosure | Plain-language disclosure, how links may work, editorial separation, program-specific details only if confirmed | ClickUp.com is the only confirmed relationship in the brief; exact legal wording remains open and other programs unconfirmed |
| 18 | Corrections / Report Error | What can be reported, working contact route, expected process only if adopted | Must be functional; response-time promises only if operationally approved |

## 6. Shared component library

Component variants alter semantic tokens and content, not core anatomy. Shared structure must not globally couple page-specific copy or product records.

| Component | Purpose / anatomy | Editable instance data | Reuse, propagation, and semantics |
|---|---|---|---|
| Header / mobile navigation | Wordmark, navigation list, search, theme control, optional CTA; mobile trigger and menu | Logo, labels, destinations, CTA visibility | Shared site shell. Propagation through verified template/theme route only; ensure nav landmarks, button trigger, expanded state, keyboard escape/return focus as implemented. |
| Button/link | Primary, secondary, commercial, text link; verb-led label | Text and destination per instance | Shared visual styles via globals/classes. Use real links for navigation and buttons for actions. Commercial variant never resembles ordinary editorial navigation. |
| Typed content card | Type label, title, summary, author/date metadata, action | Collection-specific title/summary/link/author/date/image/evidence fields | Shared anatomy with explicit article/workflow/comparison/review/tool/research variants. Each card instance owns editorial data; avoid broad global overwrites of copy. |
| Featured block | Cover/visual, label, title, summary, evidence/method, credited author, CTA | Instance-specific content, evidence, source date | Reusable shell; images must not imply testing unless true. |
| Evidence / verification badge | Text label + optional icon; neutral/status/stale | Status and record reference | Status is never color-only. “Tested”, “Verified”, “Hands-on” require linked evidence record and owner. |
| Author block | Name, role, optional genuine bio/credentials, date/method link | Author-owned identity and content metadata | A shared design pattern; no fictional credentials. Meaningful reading order and link names. |
| Affiliate disclosure | Subtle but readable editorial note and policy link | Approved disclosure text and destination | Reusable near affiliate content; wording is legal-review dependent. Never silently attach to non-affiliate links or hide it. |
| Affiliate CTA | Product/tool name, why it may fit, relevant benefit, verified information, disclosure, destination-labeled button | Verified program, destination, relation, date/source | Generic across future verified programs. Only use after value/decision support; link text names destination (e.g., “Visit [Tool] site”) and is distinguished by commercial token. |
| Verdict / best-for panel | Best for, not ideal for, trade-off, evidence note | Evidence-backed recommendation data | No numeric score unless a rubric and record support it. Empty/unavailable state is explicit. |
| Comparison option card | Option name, same rubric criteria, benefits, limitations, evidence state | Structured comparison content | Shared paired anatomy; stack on mobile, no horizontal scrolling. |
| Pros / cons | Two labeled lists with icon + text | Editorial entries | ACF Free has no repeater; keep in editor content or use bounded numbered fields only if structured reuse is justified. |
| Source/method panel | Sources, method link, tested environment/version where documented, limitations | Source URLs, method/version/data | Missing data has a defined empty rendering; never default to a fictional value. |
| Newsletter form | Benefit copy, email, consent/privacy text, feedback states | Provider-owned form, wording, policy link | Free external form integration must be selected and verified; avoid custom collection of sensitive data. |
| Breadcrumbs | Canonical hierarchy and current page | Site/page hierarchy | Use crawlable links and meaningful nav label; verify native implementation. |
| Search | Search trigger/field/results | Query and result content | Candidate widget capability is version-dependent; test empty/no-results/error behavior. |
| Methodology steps | Numbered process with concise explanation | Current editorial process | Six steps are a proposed presentation; only state process actually followed. |
| Footer | Navigation/trust/legal/corrections/newsletter | Approved routes | Shared shell with compact hierarchy; must not turn into a link farm. |

### Component ownership and propagation

- **Global owner:** design-system tokens, shared structure, accessibility states, template shell.
- **Editorial owner:** titles, summaries, articles, sources, author/date, comparison judgments, update status.
- **Commercial owner:** verified affiliate program/destination, relationship disclosure, destination status, last-check record.
- **Data owner:** structured fields, field definitions, preview context, empty states, migration/version record.
- **Propagation rule:** global visual changes may propagate; instance content and commercial/product data may not. Verify propagation behavior in the selected Elementor/theme route before relying on reusable templates or global widgets.

Verify Hello/Xpro Free route, conditions/propagation, keyboard behavior, breakpoints. |

Verify line breaks and reflow at actual + intermediate widths. |

Verify contrast and content ownership. |
| Decision cards | Route users by intent | Grid or wrapped flex | Parent container + nested card containers with Heading, Text Editor, Button/link | Static or real posts; labels, title, summary, URL | 3/2/1 conceptual columns; full-card linking only if accessible; clear focus | No Loop Grid assumed. Confirm any query/repeat workflow; otherwise compose/duplicate manually. |

Verify accordion semantics if used; no giant HTML block. |

Keep critical body server-rendered. |

Test 600–800px and zoom. |

### 7.2 Standard mapping record format

For every additional region, record: **Section; semantic purpose; layout primitive; Elementor implementation; content source; editable fields; responsive behavior; interaction states; accessibility; dependencies; exception; verification.** Do not mark candidate mappings as confirmed without target inspection.

## 8. Proposed design system and token map

All ReadMeHub tokens below are **PROPOSED**. The brief deliberately defers actual hex values, final font family, and final values pending target inspection, licensing/performance checks, and contrast measurement. Do not fill these with guessed values. Exact color values must be chosen and tested in light and dark modes before handoff to implementation.

### 8.1 Design principles

Evidence first; typed collections; one module anatomy; one primary action per view; visible commercial/editorial separation; credited authorship; token-driven restraint; deliberately designed theme parity; free-first Elementor composition. Visual tone: soft, premium, credible, editorial, clear, not a generic SaaS dashboard.

### 8.2 Token architecture and authority

**Primitive → Semantic → Component.** Define each final value once. Prefer semantic Elementor Global Colors/Fonts. Use scoped `--rmh-*` CSS variables only where native global controls cannot express the behavior. Do not duplicate literal hex/spacing values in page styles where a global reference exists.

| Token | Purpose | Canonical destination proposal | Value/status |
|---|---|---|---|
| `surface-page` | Page canvas | Elementor Global Color | Light/dark pair deferred; PROPOSED |
| `surface-raised` | Cards/panels | Elementor Global Color | Distinct by tone/border; deferred |
| `surface-inset` | Code/callout/forms | Global Color or scoped CSS var if needed | Deferred |
| `surface-inverse` | Rare conversion band | Elementor Global Color | Deferred; at most one major band per page proposed |
| `text-primary` | Main copy/headings | Elementor Global Color | Contrast target at least 4.5:1 for text; measure actual pairs |
| `text-muted` | Metadata | Elementor Global Color | Must remain readable; do not reduce small-text contrast |
| `border-subtle`, `border-strong` | Dividers/inputs | Scoped CSS variables where needed | Proposed; input border target 3:1, measure |
| `accent-primary` | Editorial links and primary actions | Elementor Global Color | One accent hue proposed; hex deferred |
| `accent-commercial` | Affiliate treatment only | Elementor Global Color | Reserved commercial role; hex deferred |
| `status-success/warning/danger/info` | State/verdict | Global colors or scoped vars | Always pair color with text/icon/label |
| `focus-ring` | Keyboard focus | Scoped CSS variable | 2px ring + offset proposed; contrast measure |
| `font-display`, `font-heading`, `font-body`, `font-label`, `font-meta`, `font-button` | Type roles | Elementor Global Fonts | Family and weights deferred; favor system stack or one suitable variable family after license/perf check |
| `space-1…9` | 4px-based spacing scale | Scoped `--rmh-space-*` vars if native spacing presets insufficient | Proposed scale 4, 8, 12, 16, 24, 32, 48, 64, 96px |
| `radius-s/m/pill` | Shape | Scoped CSS variables | 6px / 12px / 999px proposed; verify as design choice |
| `elev-1/elev-2` | Hover/menu depth | Scoped CSS classes | Two levels proposed; dark mode favors tone/border over shadow |
| `motion-fast/base` | State transitions | Scoped CSS variables | 150–250ms ease-out proposed; not an observed source value |
| `wrap/reading/wide` | Content widths | Elementor layout settings or scoped class | 1200 / 720 / 1000px proposed values from brief; validate in actual layout |

**Type scale proposal:** display H1 clamp 2.25–3.5rem; H2 1.75rem; H3 1.375rem; H4 1.125rem; body 1.0625rem with 1.65 line-height and 65–75ch reading width; card text 0.9375rem; label 0.75rem uppercase with positive tracking; metadata 0.8125rem; button 0.9375rem semibold. These are proposals, not Jentic measurements or runtime-confirmed values.

**Fluid type constraint if implemented:** `clamp(min, rem + vw, max)`, never pure `vw`; positive-size max/min ratio ≤2.5 as a project constraint. Verify 200% zoom and reflow separately. Typography fluidity is not proof of accessibility.

**Spacing/layout proposal:** 4px base scale; section rhythm roughly 64–96px desktop and one step less on mobile; content width concepts `wrap` 1200px, `wide` 1000px, `reading` 720px; 12-column framework only if useful and supported. All are proposals; actual responsive behavior must be designed at the real Site Settings breakpoints plus intermediate widths.

**Shape/depth proposal:** 1px hairline borders as primary surface separators; 2px focus/selected treatment; card radius 12px; modest 6px control radius; pill tags 999px; two elevation levels. Dark mode favors surface tones and borders, not heavy shadows.

### 8.3 Token → Elementor mapping

| Token group | Elementor / CSS destination | Status / note |
|---|---|---|
| Semantic surface/text/accent/status colors | New Kit → Global Colors, using semantic names | PROPOSED; palette values deferred and need contrast check |
| Typography roles | New Kit → Global Fonts | PROPOSED; font family/license/performance check first |
| Global button hierarchy | Global/button styles using primary, secondary, text, commercial variants | Needs target test; no duplicate page-level definitions |
| Borders/focus/radii/spacing/motion | Scoped namespace classes and CSS vars (`.rmh-*`, `--rmh-*`) only where native settings cannot express them | PROPOSED; document stylesheet and scope |
| Breakpoints | Elementor Site Settings | UNVERIFIED; inspect actual values, do not set from assumed defaults without owner approval |
| Content widths | Site settings where available, otherwise documented reusable classes | Proposed values; verify on representative pages |
